### Language

The official language is [Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabic).[^cite_note-CIA_World_Factbook-13][^cite_note-BLG-6] There are four main regional dialect groups spoken by Saudis: [Najdi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Najdi_Arabic) (about 14.6 million speakers[^cite_note-412]), [Hejazi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hejazi_Arabic) (about 10.3 million speakers[^cite_note-413]), [Gulf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gulf_Arabic) (about 0.96 million speakers[^cite_note-414]), and Southern Hejaz-Tihama[^cite_note-415] dialects. [Faifi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Faifi_language) is spoken by about 50000. The [Mehri language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mehri_language) is also spoken by around 20000 [Mehri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mehri_people) citizens.[^cite_note-416] [Saudi Sign Language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saudi_Sign_Language) is the principal language of the deaf community, amounting to around 100000 speakers. The large expatriate communities have brought their languages as well, the most numerous of which, according to 2018 data, are [Bengali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bengali_language) (\~1 500000), [Tagalog](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tagalog_language) (\~900000), [Punjabi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Punjabi_language) (\~800000), [Urdu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urdu) (\~740000), [Egyptian Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Egyptian_Arabic) (\~600000), [Rohingya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rohingya_language), [North Levantine Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Levantine_Arabic) (both \~500000)[^cite_note-417] and [Malayalam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malayalam).[^cite_note-418]

### Religion

Main article: [Religion in Saudi Arabia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religion_in_Saudi_Arabia)



[Quba Mosque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quba_Mosque) in [Medina](36636.md), [Hejaz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hejaz) is considered to be one of the [oldest mosques](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_the_oldest_mosques), dating to the lifetime of the [Islamic prophet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prophets_and_messengers_in_Islam) [Muhammad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muhammad).

[^cite_note-420]

Religion in Saudi Arabia (ARDA 2020 est.)

[^cite_note-421]

1. [Sunni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sunni) (80.1%)

2. [Shia](26961.md) (8.89%)

3. [Other Muslim](6037917.md) (0.45%)

4. [Catholic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholic) (6.52%)

5. [Other Christian](5211.md) (0.46%)

6. [Hinduism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hinduism) (2.03%)

7. [Buddhism](3267529.md) (0.33%)

8. [None](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irreligion) (0.71%)

9. Other (0.54%)

Virtually all Saudi inhabitants [are Muslim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islam_in_Saudi_Arabia);[^cite_note-422][^cite_note-PewForump.17-423][^cite_note-424] as by law Saudi citizens are Muslim. Estimates of the [Sunni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sunni) population range between 85% and 90%, with the remaining 10 to 15% being [Shia Muslim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shia_Islam_in_Saudi_Arabia),[^cite_note-PF2009-425][^cite_note-bbc-shia-426][^cite_note-cfr-shiite-427][^cite_note-Nasr2006_p._236-7] practicing either [Twelver Shi'ism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twelver_Shi'ism) or [Sulaymani Ismailism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sulaymani). Shia Muslims in Saudi Arabia are largely found in the [Eastern Province](468721.md), particularly in [Qatif](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qatif) and [Al–Ahsa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Ahsa_Governorate).[^cite_note-ibnghannam-428] The official and dominant form of Sunni Islam is [Salafism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salafism), commonly known as [Wahhabism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wahhabism),[^cite_note-429][^cite_note-The_Daily_Star-430][^cite_note-431] which was founded in the Arabian Peninsula by [Muhammad ibn Abd al-Wahhab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muhammad_ibn_Abd_al-Wahhab) in the 18th century. Other denominations, including [Shia Islam](26961.md), are systematically suppressed by the state apparatus.[^cite_note-432] Atheism and apostasy are punishable by death or by life imprisonment.

[Christianity is the second largest religion in Saudi Arabia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christianity_in_Saudi_Arabia), estimated at around 2.1 million, of whom the vast majority are foreign workers.[^cite_note-433] Saudi Arabia allows Christians to enter the country as temporary foreign workers but does not allow them to practice their faith openly. There are officially no Saudi citizens who are Christians,[^cite_note-434] as Saudi Arabia forbids religious conversion from Islam ([apostasy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apostasy_in_Saudi_Arabia)) and punishes it by death.[^cite_note-435] There are over 708,000 [Hindus in Saudi Arabia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hinduism_in_Saudi_Arabia), comprising the third largest religion; like other minority faiths in the country, with all of them coming from South Asia.[^cite_note-436] There may be a significant fraction of [atheists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atheism) and [agnostics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agnosticism),[^cite_note-437][^cite_note-438] although they are officially designated as "terrorists".[^cite_note-439] In its 2017 religious freedom report, the U.S. State Department named Saudi Arabia a [Country of Particular Concern](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Country_of_Particular_Concern), denoting systematic, ongoing, and egregious violations of religious freedom.[^cite_note-440]

[Najran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Najran) was once home to sizeable historical local [Christian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian_community_of_Najran) and [Jewish communities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_Jews_in_Saudi_Arabia).[^cite_note-\:2-441] Before the founding of [Israel](9282173.md), Najran had over 7,000 Jews,[^cite_note-442][^cite_note-\:2-441] predominantly of [Yemenite Jewish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yemenite_Jews) background.[^cite_note-\:2-441] Following the [establishment of Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Establishment_of_Israel) and the [1948 Arab–Israeli war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1948_Arab%E2%80%93Israeli_War), Jews began leaving for Israel via [Yemen](350939.md).[^cite_note-\:2-441] By the 1970s, there were no Jews left in the country.

### Education

Main article: [Education in Saudi Arabia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Education_in_Saudi_Arabia)



Laboratory buildings at [KAUST](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KAUST)

Education is free at all levels, although [higher education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Higher_education_in_Saudi_Arabia) is restricted to citizens only.[^cite_note-443] The school system is composed of [elementary, intermediate, and secondary schools](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_schools_in_Saudi_Arabia). Classes are segregated by sex. At the secondary level, students are able to choose from three types of schools: general education, vocational or [technical](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_technical_colleges_in_Saudi_Arabia), and religious.[^cite_note-444] The rate of literacy is 99% among males and 96% among females in 2020.[^cite_note-445][^cite_note-446] Youth literacy rose to approximately 99.5% for both sexes.[^cite_note-447][^cite_note-448]



The entrance gate of [King Saud University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/King_Saud_University), the kingdom's oldest university, founded in 1957

Higher education has expanded rapidly, with large numbers of [universities and colleges being founded particularly since 2000](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_universities_and_colleges_in_Saudi_Arabia). Institutions of higher education include [King Saud University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/King_Saud_University) at Riyadh, the [Islamic University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_University_of_Madinah) at Medina, and the [King Abdulaziz University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/King_Abdulaziz_University) in Jeddah. [Princess Norah University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Princess_Nora_bint_Abdul_Rahman_University) is the largest women's university in the world. [King Abdullah University of Science and Technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/King_Abdullah_University_of_Science_and_Technology), known as KAUST, is the first mixed-gender university campus in Saudi Arabia and was founded in 2009. Other colleges and universities emphasize curricula in sciences and [technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Educational_technology_in_Saudi_Arabia), military studies, religion, and medicine. Institutes devoted to Islamic studies, in particular, abound. Women typically receive college instruction in segregated institutions.[^cite_note-Britannica-146]



[UIS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UNESCO_Institute_for_Statistics) literacy rate Saudi Arabia population, 15 plus, 1990–2015

The *[Academic Ranking of World Universities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academic_Ranking_of_World_Universities)*, known as Shanghai Ranking, ranked five Saudi institutions among its 2022 list of the 500 top universities in the world.[^cite_note-449] The [QS World University Rankings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/QS_World_University_Rankings) lists 14 Saudi universities among the 2022 world's top universities and 23 universities among the top 100 in the [Arab world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_world).[^cite_note-450] The 2022 list of [U.S. News & World Report Best Global University Ranking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._News_&_World_Report_Best_Global_University_Ranking) ranked King Abdulaziz University among the top 50 universities in the world and [KAUST](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KAUST) among the top 100 universities in the world.[^cite_note-451]

In 2018, Saudi Arabia ranked 28th worldwide in terms of high-quality research output according to the scientific journal *[Nature](43427.md).*[^cite_note-452] Saudi Arabia spends 9% of its gross domestic product on education, compared with the global average of 5%.[^cite_note-453] Saudi Arabia was ranked 46th in the [Global Innovation Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Innovation_Index) in 2025, up from 68th in 2019.[^cite_note-454][^cite_note-455][^cite_note-456][^cite_note-457]

The Saudi education system has been accused of encouraging [Islamic terrorism](566821.md), leading to reform efforts.[^cite_note-Reforming_Saudi_Education-458][^cite_note-459] Following the 9/11 attacks, the government aimed to tackle the twin problems of encouraging extremism and the inadequacy of the country's university education for a modern economy, by slowly modernizing the education system through the "Tatweer" reform programme.[^cite_note-Reforming_Saudi_Education-458] The Tatweer programme is reported to have a budget of approximately US\$2 billion and focuses on moving teaching away from the traditional Saudi methods of memorization and rote learning towards encouraging students to analyse and problem-solve. It also aims to create an education system which will provide a more secular and vocationally based training.[^cite_note-chronicle.com-460][^cite_note-461]

In 2021, the *[Washington Post](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Washington_Post)* reported on the measures taken by Saudi Arabia to clean textbooks from paragraphs considered [antisemitic](1078.md) and [sexist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sexism). The paragraphs dealing with the punishment of [homosexuality](5488304.md) or same-sex relations have been deleted, as well as the expressions of admiration for the extremist martyrdom. Antisemitic expressions and calls to fight the Jews became fewer. David Weinberg, director of international affairs for the [Anti-Defamation League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-Defamation_League), said that references to demonizing Jews, Christians, and Shiites have been removed from some places or have toned down. The U.S. State Department expressed in an e-mail that it welcomed the changes. The Saudi Ministry of Foreign Affairs supports a training programme for Saudi teachers.[^cite_note-462]

### Health care

Main articles: [Health in Saudi Arabia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Health_in_Saudi_Arabia) and [Health care in Saudi Arabia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Health_care_in_Saudi_Arabia)



Saudi twins receiving care from doctors at [King Abdulaziz Medical City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/King_Abdulaziz_Medical_City) in [Riyadh](51554.md)

Saudi Arabia has a national health care system in which the government provides free health care services through government agencies. Saudi Arabia has been ranked among the 26 best countries in providing high quality healthcare.[^cite_note-463] The [Ministry of Health](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Health_\(Saudi_Arabia\)) is the major government agency entrusted with the provision of preventive, curative, and rehabilitative health care. The ministry's origins can be traced to 1925, when several regional health departments were established, with the first in Makkah. The various healthcare institutions were merged to become a ministerial body in 1950.[^cite_note-464] The Health Ministry created a friendly competition between each of the districts and between different medical services and hospitals. This idea resulted in the creation of the "Ada'a" project launched in 2016. The new system is a nationwide performance indicator, for services and hospitals. Waiting times and other major measurements improved dramatically across the kingdom.[^cite_note-465]



Historical development of life expectancy in Saudi Arabia

A new strategy has been developed by the ministry, known as Diet and Physical Activity Strategy or DPAS for short,[^cite_note-466] to address bad lifestyle choices. The ministry advised that there should be a tax increase on unhealthy food, drink, and cigarettes. This additional tax could be used to improve healthcare offerings. The tax was implemented in 2017.[^cite_note-467] As part of the same strategy, calorie labels were added in 2019 to some food and drink products. Ingredients were also listed as an aim to reduce obesity and inform citizens with health issues to manage their diet.[^cite_note-468] As part of the ongoing focus on tackling obesity, women-only gyms were allowed to open in 2017. Sports offered in each of these gyms include bodybuilding, running and swimming to maintain higher standards of health.[^cite_note-469][^cite_note-470]

[Smoking in all age groups](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Smoking_in_Saudi_Arabia) is widespread. Before 2010, Saudi Arabia had no policies banning or restricting smoking.

The MOH has been awarded "Healthy City" certificates by the [World Health Organization](33583.md) (WHO) for the cities of [Unayzah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unayzah) and [Riyadh Al Khabra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Riyadh_Al_Khabra) as 4th and 5th Healthy Cities in Saudi Arabia.[^cite_note-471] The WHO had earlier classified three Saudi Arabian cities, [Ad Diriyah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ad_Diriyah), [Jalajil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jalajil), and Al-Jamoom as "Healthy city", as part of the WHO Healthy Cities Programme. Recently [Al-Baha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Baha) has also been classified as a healthy city to join the list of global healthy cities approved by the World Health Organization.[^cite_note-472]

In May 2019, the then Saudi Minister of Health [Tawfiq bin Fawzan AlRabiah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tawfiq_bin_Fawzan_AlRabiah) received a global award on behalf of the Kingdom for combating smoking through social awareness, treatment, and application of regulations.[^cite_note-smokingaward-473] The award was presented as part of the 72nd session of the [World Health Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Health_Assembly), held in Geneva in May 2019. After becoming one of the first nations to ratify the [WHO Framework Convention on Tobacco Control](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WHO_Framework_Convention_on_Tobacco_Control) in 2005, it plans to reduce tobacco use from 12.7% in 2017, to 5% in 2030.[^cite_note-smokingaward-473]

Saudi Arabia has a life expectancy of 78 years (77 for males and 80 for females) according to the latest data for the year 2022 from the World Bank.[^cite_note-\:1-474] Infant mortality in 2022 was 6 per 1000 (6 for males and 5 for females).[^cite_note-\:1-474] In 2022, 71.8% of the adult population was overweight and 40.6% was obese.[^cite_note-475]

### Foreigners

See also: [Foreign workers in Saudi Arabia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_workers_in_Saudi_Arabia), [Migrant workers in the Gulf region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Migrant_workers_in_the_Gulf_region), and [Kafala system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kafala_system)

The Central Department of Statistics & Information estimated the foreign population at the end of 2014 at 33% (10.1 million).[^cite_note-CDSI-476] The CIA Factbook estimated that as of 2013 foreign nationals living in Saudi Arabia made up about 21% of the population.[^cite_note-CIA_World_Factbook-13] Other sources report differing estimates.[^cite_note-alriyadh.com-477] [Indian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indians_in_Saudi_Arabia): 1.5 million, [Pakistani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pakistanis_in_Saudi_Arabia): 1.3 million,[^cite_note-478] Egyptian: 900000, Yemeni: 800000, [Bangladeshi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bangladeshis_in_Saudi_Arabia): 400000, [Filipino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Filipinos_in_Saudi_Arabia): 500000, Jordanian/Palestinian: 260000, [Indonesian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesians_in_Saudi_Arabia): 250000, Sri Lankan: 350000, Sudanese: 250000, [Syrian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrians_in_Saudi_Arabia): 100000 and [Turkish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turks_in_Saudi_Arabia): 80000.[^cite_note-479]

According to *[The Guardian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Guardian)*, as of 2013 there were more than half a million [foreign-born domestic workers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Migrant_domestic_workers). Most have backgrounds in poverty and come from Africa, the Indian subcontinent and Southeast Asia.[^cite_note-the_guardian1-480] To go to work in Saudi Arabia, they must often pay large sums to recruitment agencies in their home countries. The agencies then handle the necessary legal paperwork.[^cite_note-humanrightswatch1-481]

As the Saudi population grows and oil export revenues stagnate, pressure for "Saudization" (the replacement of foreign workers with Saudis) has grown, and the Saudi government hopes to decrease the number of foreign nationals in the country.[^cite_note-482] Saudi Arabia expelled 800000 Yemenis in 1990 and 1991[^cite_note-483] and has built a [Saudi–Yemen barrier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saudi%E2%80%93Yemen_barrier) against an influx of illegal immigrants and against the smuggling of drugs and weapons.[^cite_note-484] In November 2013, Saudi Arabia expelled thousands of illegal Ethiopian residents from the kingdom. Various Human Rights entities have criticized Saudi Arabia's handling of the issue.[^cite_note-485]

Over 500000 [undocumented migrant workers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illegal_immigration_to_Saudi_Arabia)—mostly from Somalia, Ethiopia, and Yemen—have been detained and deported since 2013.[^cite_note-486] An investigation led by *[The Sunday Telegraph](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Sunday_Telegraph)*, exposed the condition of African migrants who were detained in Saudi Arabia allegedly for containing [COVID-19](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coronavirus_disease_2019) in the kingdom. They were beaten, tortured, and electrocuted. Many of the migrants died due to heatstroke or by attempting suicide, after being severely beaten and tortured. The migrants lack proper living conditions, provision of food and water.[^cite_note-487]

Foreigners cannot apply for [permanent residency](1284324.md), though a specialized [Premium Residency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Premium_Residency) visa became available in 2019.[^cite_note-488] Only Muslims can naturalize as Saudi citizens.[^cite_note-489] Foreigners who have resided in the kingdom and hold degrees in various scientific fields may apply for Saudi citizenship,[^cite_note-490][^cite_note-491] and exception made for Palestinians who are excluded unless married to a male Saudi national, because of [Arab League](52625.md) instructions barring the Arab states from granting them citizenship. Saudi Arabia is not a signatory to the [1951 UN Refugee Convention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Convention_relating_to_the_Status_of_Refugees).[^cite_note-492]

## Culture

Main article: [Culture of Saudi Arabia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_Saudi_Arabia)



The [Mosque of the Prophet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mosque_of_the_Prophet) in [Medina](36636.md) containing the tomb of [Muhammad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muhammad)

Saudi Arabia has millennia-old attitudes and traditions, often derived from [Arab civilization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_Arabs). Some of the major factors that influence the culture are Islamic heritage and [Arab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab) traditions as well as its historical role as an ancient trade centre.[^cite_note-493] The Kingdom also has a very family-oriented culture[^cite_note-494] with an emphasis on preserving family traditions and kinship ties.[^cite_note-495]

### Religion in society

Religion is a core aspect of everyday life in Saudi Arabia; it plays a dominant role in the country's governance and legal system, and deeply influences culture and daily life, although the power of the religious establishment has been significantly eroded in the 2010s.[^cite_note-\:3-496] The [Hejaz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hejaz) region, where the [Islamic holy cities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holiest_sites_in_Islam) of Mecca and Medina are located, is the destination of the [Ḥajj](23487999.md) pilgrimage, and often deemed to be the cradle of Islam.[^cite_note-Arabia:_the_Cradle_of_Islam-497][^cite_note-508]

Islam is the state religion of Saudi Arabia. There is no law that requires all citizens to be Muslim, but non-Muslims and many foreign and Saudi Muslims whose beliefs are deemed not to conform with the government's interpretation of Islam must practice their religion in private and are vulnerable to discrimination, harassment, detention, and, for foreigners, deportation.[^cite_note-509] Neither Saudi citizens nor guest workers have the right of [freedom of religion](46006.md).[^cite_note-depstate-510] The dominant form of Islam in the kingdom—Wahhabism—arose in the central region of Najd, in the 18th century. Proponents call the movement "[Salafism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salafism)",[^cite_note-The_Daily_Star-430] and believe that its teachings purify the practice of Islam of innovations or practices that deviate from the seventh-century teachings of Muhammad and [his companions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sahaba).[^cite_note-511] The Saudi government has often been viewed as an active oppressor of [Shia Muslims](26961.md) because of the funding of the Wahhabi ideology which denounces the Shia faith.[^cite_note-512][^cite_note-syedjaffar-513] Prince [Bandar bin Sultan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bandar_bin_Sultan), Saudi ambassador to the United States, stated: "The time is not far off in the Middle East when it will be literally 'God help the Shia'. More than a billion Sunnis have simply had enough of them."[^cite_note-514]



Supplicating [pilgrim](23763.md#Islam) at *[Al-Masjid Al-Ḥarām](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Mosque_of_Mecca)* (The Sacred Mosque) in [Mecca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mecca). The [Kaaba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaaba) (the holiest site of Islam) is the cubic building in front of the pilgrim.

Saudi Arabia is one of the few countries that have "[religious police](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Committee_for_the_Promotion_of_Virtue_and_the_Prevention_of_Vice_\(Saudi_Arabia\))" (known as *Haia* or *Mutaween*), who patrol the streets "[enjoining good and forbidding wrong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enjoining_good_and_forbidding_wrong)" by enforcing [dress codes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clothing_laws_by_country), strict [separation of men and women](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sex_segregation), attendance at prayer (*[salat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salat)*) five times each day, the ban on alcohol, and other aspects of *Sharia.* However, since 2016 the power of religious police was curbed, which barred them from pursuing, questioning, requesting identification or arresting suspects.[^cite_note-france24.com-515][^cite_note-\:4-268] In the privacy of homes, behaviour can be far looser, and reports from [WikiLeaks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WikiLeaks) indicate that low ranked members of the ruling Saudi Royal family indulge in parties with alcohol, drugs, and prostitutes.[^cite_note-516]

### Women in society

See also: [Women's rights in Saudi Arabia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Women's_rights_in_Saudi_Arabia) and [Women's education in Saudi Arabia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Women's_education_in_Saudi_Arabia)

Throughout history, women did not have equal rights to men in the kingdom; the [U.S. State Department](31975.md) considers the Saudi Arabian government's discrimination against women a "significant problem" and notes that women have few political rights because of the government's discriminatory policies.[^cite_note-State2010-517] However, since 2017, a series of social reforms have been witnessed regarding women's rights.

Under previous Saudi law, all females were required to have a male guardian (*[wali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wali_\(Islamic_legal_guardian\))*), typically a father, brother, husband, or uncle (*mahram*). In 2019, this law was partially amended to exclude women over 21 years old from the requirement of a male guardian.[^cite_note-\:22-518] The amendment also granted women rights in relation to the guardianship of minor children.[^cite_note-\:22-518][^cite_note-\:32-519] Previously, girls and women were forbidden from travelling, conducting official business, or undergoing certain medical procedures without permission from their male guardians.[^cite_note-hrw-permission-520] In 2019, Saudi Arabia allowed women to travel abroad, register for divorce or marriage, and apply for official documents without the permission of a male guardian.

In 2006, [Wajeha al-Huwaider](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wajeha_al-Huwaider), a leading Saudi feminist and journalist said "Saudi women are weak, no matter how high their status, even the 'pampered' ones among them, because they have no law to protect them from attack by anyone."[^cite_note-521] Following this, Saudi Arabia implemented the anti-[domestic violence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Domestic_violence_in_Saudi_Arabia) law in 2014.[^cite_note-522] Furthermore, between 2017 and 2020, the country addressed issues of mobility, sexual harassment, pensions, and employment-discrimination protections.[^cite_note-523][^cite_note-524][^cite_note-525] al-Huwaider and other female activists have applauded the general direction in which the country was headed.[^cite_note-526]



[Princess Reema bint Bandar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reema_bint_Bandar_Al_Saud), the [Saudi Ambassador to the United States of America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_ambassadors_of_Saudi_Arabia_to_the_United_States), delivering an address at an event honouring the 75th anniversary of [Saudi-US relations](9753135.md)

Women face discrimination in the courts, where the testimony of one man equals that of two women [in family and inheritance law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legal_system_of_Saudi_Arabia#Family_law).[^cite_note-State2010-517] Polygamy is permitted for men,[^cite_note-527] and men have a unilateral right to divorce their wives ([talaq](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Divorce_\(Islamic\))) without needing any legal justification.[^cite_note-Otto164-528] Historically, a woman could only obtain a divorce with the consent of her husband or judicially if her husband has harmed her;[^cite_note-Otto163-529] however, in 2022, women were granted the right to initiate divorce without the approval of a legal guardian under the new Personal Status Law.[^cite_note-530] With regard to inheritance law, the Quran specifies that fixed portions of the deceased's estate must be left to the *Qur'anic heirs*[^cite_note-Otto165-531] and generally, female heirs receive half the portion of male heirs.[^cite_note-Otto165-531]

### Heritage sites

See also: [Mecca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mecca), [Medina](36636.md), [Destruction of early Islamic heritage sites in Saudi Arabia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Destruction_of_early_Islamic_heritage_sites_in_Saudi_Arabia), [Tourism in Saudi Arabia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourism_in_Saudi_Arabia), and [Saudi Heritage Preservation Society](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saudi_Heritage_Preservation_Society)



The 3000-year-old ancient historical city of [Dumat al-Jandal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dumat_al-Jandal) in [Al Jawf Province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Jawf_Province)

&#x20;Saudi Wahhabism is hostile to any reverence given to historical or religious places of significance for fear that it may give rise to ['shirk'](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shirk_\(Islam\)) (idolatry), and the most significant historic Muslim sites (in Mecca and Medina) are located in the western Saudi region of the Hejaz.[^cite_note-Arabia:_the_Cradle_of_Islam-497] As a consequence, under Saudi rule an estimated 95% of Mecca's historic buildings, most over a thousand years old, [have been demolished](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Destruction_of_early_Islamic_heritage_sites_in_Saudi_Arabia) for religious reasons.[^cite_note-532] Critics claim that over the last 50 years, 300 historic sites linked to Muhammad, his family or companions have been lost,[^cite_note-533] leaving fewer than 20 structures remaining in Mecca that date back to the time of Muhammad.[^cite_note-independent.co.uk-534] Demolished structures include the mosque originally built by Muhammad's daughter [Fatima](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fatima_bint_Muhammad), and other mosques founded by [Abu Bakr](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abu_Bakr) (Muhammad's father-in-law and the first [caliph](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caliph)), [Umar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Umar_ibn_al-Khattab) (the second caliph), [Ali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ali_ibn_Abi_Talib) (Muhammad's son-in-law and the fourth caliph), and [Salman al-Farsi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salman_al-Farsi) (another of Muhammad's companions).[^cite_note-535] Other historic buildings that have been destroyed include the house of [Khadijah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khadija_bint_Khuwaylid), the wife of Muhammad, the house of Abu Bakr, now the site of the local [Hilton hotel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hilton_hotel); the house of Ali-Oraid, the grandson of Muhammad, and the Mosque of Abu-Qubais, now the location of the King's palace in Mecca.[^cite_note-536]



The [Mosque of the Prophet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mosque_of_the_Prophet) in [Medina](36636.md) containing the tomb of [Muhammad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muhammad)

Eight cultural sites in Saudi Arabia are designated as [UNESCO World Heritage Sites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UNESCO_World_Heritage_Sites): Al-Hijr Archaeological Site ([Madâin Sâlih](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mada'in_Saleh));[^cite_note-537] the [Turaif district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turaif_district) in Diriyah;[^cite_note-538] Historic Jeddah, the Gate to Mecca;[^cite_note-539] [Al-Ahsa Oasis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Ahsa_Oasis);[^cite_note-540] [Rock Art in the Hail Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rock_Art_in_the_Ha'il_Region);[^cite_note-541] [Ḥimā Cultural Area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bir_Hima_Rock_Petroglyphs_and_Inscriptions);[^cite_note-542] ['Uruq Bani Ma'arid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/'Uruq_Bani_Ma'arid);[^cite_note-543] and [Qaryat al-Faw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qaryat_al-Faw). Ten other sites submitted requests for recognition to UNESCO in 2015.[^cite_note-544] There are six elements inscribed on [UNESCO's Intangible Cultural Heritage of Humanity list](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UNESCO_Intangible_Cultural_Heritage_Lists):[^cite_note-545] [Al-Qatt Al-Asiri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Qatt_Al-Asiri), female traditional interior wall decoration in Asir; [Almezmar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Almezmar_in_Saudi_Arabia), drumming and dancing with sticks; [Falconry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Falconry), a living human heritage; [Arabic coffee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabic_coffee), a symbol of generosity; [Majlis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Majlis), a cultural and social space; [Alardah Alnajdiyah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alardah_Alnajdiyah), dance, drumming and poetry in Saudi Arabia.

In June 2014, the Council of Ministers approved a law that gives the [Saudi Commission for Tourism and National Heritage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saudi_Commission_for_Tourism_and_National_Heritage) the means to protect Saudi Arabia's ancient relics and historic sites. Within the framework of the 2016 National Transformation Programme, also known as [Saudi Vision 2030](50299103.md), the kingdom allocated 900 million euros to preserve its historical and cultural heritage.[^cite_note-546] Saudi Arabia also participates in the International Alliance for the Protection of Heritage in Conflict Areas, created in March 2017, with a contribution of 18.5 million euros.[^cite_note-547]

In 2017, Crown Prince Mohammad bin Salman promised to return Saudi Arabia to the "moderate Islam" of the era before the 1979 Iranian revolution.[^cite_note-548] A new centre, the King Salman Complex for the Prophet's Hadith, was established that year to monitor interpretations of Muhammad's hadiths to prevent them being used to justifying terrorism.[^cite_note-549]

In March 2018, the Crown Prince met the Archbishop of Canterbury during a visit to the UK, pledging to promote interfaith dialogue. In Riyadh the following month King Salman met the head of the Vatican's Pontifical Council for Interreligious Dialogue.[^cite_note-550] In July 2019, UNESCO signed a letter with the Saudi Minister of Culture in which Saudi Arabia contributed US\$25 million to UNESCO for the preservation of heritage.[^cite_note-551]

On November 5, 2024, archeologists published the news of an ancient city discovered in the Saudi oasis of [Khaybar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khaybar). The city named [al-Natah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Natah), that dates back some 4,000 years, was inhabited during the Bronze Age around 2,400 BCE, and had about 500 houses.[^cite_note-552]

### Dress

Saudi Arabian dress strictly follows [hijab](68301.md) (the Islamic principle of modesty, especially in dress). The predominantly loose and flowing, but covering, garments are suited to Saudi Arabia's desert climate. Traditionally, men usually wear a white ankle-length garment woven from wool or cotton (known as a [thawb](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thawb)), with a [keffiyeh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Keffiyeh) (a large checkered square of cotton held in place by an [agal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agal_\(accessory\))) or a ghutra (a plain white square made of a finer cotton, also held in place by an agal) worn on the head. For rare chilly days, Saudi men wear a camel-hair cloak ([bisht](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bisht_\(clothing\))) over the top. In public, women are required to wear a black [abaya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abaya) or other black clothing that covers everything under the neck with the exception of their hands and feet, although most women cover their head for religious reasons. This requirement applies to non-Muslim women too and failure to abide can result in police action, particularly in more conservative areas. Women's clothes are often decorated with tribal motifs, coins, sequins, metallic thread, and appliques.[^cite_note-553]

### Arts and entertainment

Main articles: [Saudi Arabian art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saudi_Arabian_art), [Cinema of Saudi Arabia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cinema_of_Saudi_Arabia), [Music of Saudi Arabia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_of_Saudi_Arabia), and [Theatre in Saudi Arabia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theatre_in_Saudi_Arabia)



King Abdullah practising [falconry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Falconry), a traditional pursuit in the country

During the 1970s, cinemas were numerous in the kingdom although they were seen as contrary to Wahhabi norms.[^cite_note-Return_of_cinema_in_Saudi_Arabia_provokes_critics-554] During the [Islamic revival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_revival) movement in the 1980s, and as a political response to an increase in Islamist activism including the 1979 seizure of the Grand Mosque in Mecca, the government closed all cinemas and theatres. However, with King Abdullah and King Salman's reforms, cinemas re-opened,[^cite_note-555] including one in [KAUST](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/King_Abdullah_University_of_Science_and_Technology).

From the 18th century onward, Wahhabi fundamentalism discouraged artistic development inconsistent with its teaching. In addition, Sunni Islamic prohibition of creating representations of people have limited the visual arts, which tend to be dominated by [geometric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_geometric_patterns), [floral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabesque), and abstract designs and by [calligraphy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_calligraphy). With the advent of the oil-wealth in the 20th century came exposure to outside influences, such as Western housing styles, furnishings, and clothes. Music and dance have always been part of Saudi life. Traditional music is generally associated with poetry and is sung collectively. Instruments include the [rabābah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rebab), an instrument not unlike a three-string fiddle, and various types of percussion instruments, such as the ṭabl (drum) and the ṭār (tambourine). The national dance is a native sword dance known as [ardah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ardah). Originating from Najd, it involves lines or circles of men and singing poetry.[^cite_note-556] Bedouin poetry, known as nabaṭī, is popular.[^cite_note-Britannica-146]

Censorship has limited the development of Saudi literature, although several [Saudi novelists and poets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Saudi_Arabian_writers) have achieved critical and popular acclaim in the Arab world—albeit generating official hostility in their home country. These include [Ghazi Algosaibi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ghazi_Algosaibi), [Mansour al-Nogaidan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mansour_al-Nogaidan), [Abdelrahman Munif](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdelrahman_Munif), [Turki al-Hamad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turki_al-Hamad), and [Rajaa al-Sanea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rajaa_al-Sanea).[^cite_note-Guardian2410-557][^cite_note-558][^cite_note-559] In 2016, the General Entertainment Authority (GEA) was formed to oversee the expansion of the Saudi entertainment sector.[^cite_note-560] The first concerts in Riyadh for 25 years took place the following year.[^cite_note-561] In 2018 the first public cinema opened after a ban of 35 years, with plans to have more than 2000 screens running by 2030.[^cite_note-562]

Developments in the arts in 2018 included Saudi Arabia's debut appearances at the [Cannes Film Festival](101460.md) and the [Venice Biennale](301876.md).[^cite_note-563]

### TV and media

Main articles: [Mass media in Saudi Arabia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mass_media_in_Saudi_Arabia) and [Television in Saudi Arabia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Television_in_Saudi_Arabia)



[Saad Khader](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saad_Khader) (left) and [Mohammad Al-Ali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammad_Al-Ali) (right) in 1979

Television was introduced in Saudi Arabia in 1954. Saudi Arabia is a major market for [pan-Arab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pan-Arab_States) satellite and [pay-TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pay_television). It controls the largest share of the pan-Arab broadcasting market; among the major Saudi-owned broadcasting companies are the [Middle East Broadcasting Centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MBC_Group), [Rotana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rotana_Group), and the [Saudi Broadcasting Authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saudi_Broadcasting_Authority).[^cite_note-obg-564] The Saudi government [closely monitors media and restricts it](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Censorship_in_Saudi_Arabia) under official state law. Changes have been made to lessen these restrictions; however, some government-led efforts to control information have also drawn international attention. As of 2022, [Reporters Without Borders](526054.md) rates the kingdom's press a "very serious" situation.[^cite_note-565]

Most of the early newspapers in the Persian Gulf region were established in Saudi Arabia.[^cite_note-aarti-566] The first newspaper founded in the country and in the Persian Gulf area is *[Al Fallah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Fallah)*, which was launched in 1920,[^cite_note-aarti-566] and the first English-language newspaper is *[Arab News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_News)*, which was launched in 1975.[^cite_note-567] All of the [newspapers published in Saudi Arabia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_newspapers_in_Saudi_Arabia) are privately owned.[^cite_note-568]

According to [World Bank](36752.md), as of 2020, 98% of the population of Saudi Arabia are internet users which puts it in the 8th rank among countries with the highest percentage of internet users.[^cite_note-569] Saudi Arabia has one of the fastest [5G](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/5G) internet speeds in the world.[^cite_note-570][^cite_note-571] The kingdom is the 27th largest market for [e-commerce](9611.md) with a revenue of US\$8 billion in 2021.[^cite_note-572]

### Cuisine

Main articles: [Saudi Arabian cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saudi_Arabian_cuisine) and [Arab cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_cuisine)



[Arabic coffee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabic_coffee) is a traditional beverage in Arabian cuisine.

Saudi Arabian cuisine is similar to that of the surrounding countries in the Arabian Peninsula and the wider Arab world, and has influenced and been influenced by Turkish, Indian, Persian, and African food. [Islamic dietary laws](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_dietary_laws) are enforced: pork is not allowed, and other animals are slaughtered in accordance with [halal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Halal). [Kebabs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kebab) and [falafel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Falafel) are popular, as is [shawarma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shawarma), a marinated grilled meat dish of lamb, mutton, or chicken. [Kabsa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kabsa), a rice dish with lamb, chicken, fish, or shrimp, is among the national dishes as is [mandi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mandi_\(food\)). Flat, unleavened [taboon bread](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taboon_bread) is a staple of virtually every meal, as are dates, fresh fruit, yoghurt, and hummus. Coffee, served in the [Arabic style](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabic_coffee), is the traditional beverage, but tea and various fruit juices are popular as well.[^cite_note-Britannica-146] The earliest substantiated evidence of either [coffee drinking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_coffee) or knowledge of the coffee tree is from the 15th century, in the [Sufi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sufi) monasteries of Arabia.

### Sport

Main article: [Sport in Saudi Arabia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sport_in_Saudi_Arabia)

See also: [Saudi Arabia at the Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saudi_Arabia_at_the_Olympics)

#### [Association football](10568.md) / soccer



[King Fahd International Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/King_Fahd_International_Stadium) in [Riyadh](51554.md)

[Association football](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Football_in_Saudi_Arabia) is the national sport of Saudi Arabia. The [Saudi Arabia national football team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saudi_Arabia_national_football_team) is among Asia's strongest, having reached a joint record six [AFC Asian Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AFC_Asian_Cup) finals, winning three of those finals ([1984](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1984_AFC_Asian_Cup_final), [1988](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1988_AFC_Asian_Cup_final), and [1996](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1996_AFC_Asian_Cup_final)) and [having qualified for the World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saudi_Arabia_at_the_FIFA_World_Cup) four consecutive times ever since debuting at the [1994 tournament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1994_FIFA_World_Cup). In the 1994 FIFA World Cup under the leadership of [Jorge Solari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jorge_Solari), Saudi Arabia beat both [Belgium](679716.md) and [Morocco](1024865.md) in the group stage before failing to defeat [Sweden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sweden_men's_national_football_team) in the round of 16. During the [1992 FIFA Confederations Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1992_King_Fahd_Cup), which was played in Saudi Arabia, the country reached the [final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1992_King_Fahd_Cup_Final), losing 1–3 to [Argentina](454699.md). Since 2023, Saudi Arabia's [Public Investment Fund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_Investment_Fund) has brought many elite footballers to the country's league, such as [Cristiano Ronaldo](623737.md), [Roberto Firmino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roberto_Firmino), [Neymar](15326144.md), [Sadio Mané](34758489.md), [Jordan Henderson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jordan_Henderson) and [Kalidou Koulibaly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kalidou_Koulibaly). Even domestic competitions from Spain and Italy have been held in Saudi Arabia during the 2020s. In December 2024, Saudi Arabia was confirmed as host of the [2034 FIFA World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2034_FIFA_World_Cup).[^cite_note-573] There are reportedly 11 new stadiums being built and airports are scheduled to be expanded to accommodate the expected influx of passengers.[^cite_note-574]

##### National league

*See also: [Saudi Pro League](4525043.md)*

The [Saudi Pro League](4525043.md) was established in 1957,[^cite_note-j-575] and the tournament has been running ever since. The league had prior names like His Majesty's League and The King's Cup in the late 1950s and early 1960s. The first season in 1975/76 had 8 clubs and was abandoned after only 4 rounds, presumably after the assassination of [King Faisal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Faisal_of_Saudi_Arabia).[^cite_note-\:9-576] The subsequent 1976/77 season also had 8 clubs, then 10 teams with 2 [relegating](648049.md), then 20 teams with 5 relegating, then 10 teams with 2 relegating again, and then 12 teams. The [Saudi Arabian Football Federation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saudi_Arabian_Football_Federation) (SAFF) then increased the number of clubs to 14 in the 2010/11 season, 16 in 2018/19 and finally 18 in the recent 2023/24 season.[^cite_note-\:9-576] The most dominant club in the Saudi Pro League for men is [Al-Hilal SFC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Hilal_SFC) with 21 titles, followed by [Al-Ittihad Club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Ittihad_Club_\(Jeddah\)) with 14 trophies, [Al-Nassr FC](2535118.md) with 11 Saudi Pro Leagues and [Al-Ahli Saudi FC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Ahli_Saudi_FC) with 9 tournaments in second, third, and fourth respectively in terms of the number of trophies won by one specific club.[^cite_note-\:9-576][^cite_note-577]

#### Other sports

[Scuba diving](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scuba_diving), [windsurfing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Windsurfing), [sailing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sailing), and [basketball](3921.md) (which is played by both men and women) are also popular with the [Saudi Arabian national basketball team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saudi_Arabia_national_basketball_team) winning bronze at the [1999 Asian Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1999_ABC_Championship).[^cite_note-578][^cite_note-579][^cite_note-580] More traditional sports such as [horse racing](74711.md) and [camel racing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Camel_racing) are also popular. The annual King's Camel Race, which began in 1974, is one of the sport's most important contests and attracts animals and riders from throughout the region. [Falconry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Falconry) is another traditional pursuit.[^cite_note-Britannica-146]



[Sarah Attar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sarah_Attar) competing at the [2012 Summer Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_Summer_Olympics) as one of the first two females representing Saudi Arabia

[Women's sport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Women's_sport_in_Saudi_Arabia) is controversial because of the suppression of female participation in sport by conservative Islamic religious authorities,[^cite_note-581] however the restrictions have eased.[^cite_note-582][^cite_note-583][^cite_note-584] Until 2018 women were not permitted in sport stadiums. Segregated seating, allowing women to enter, has been developed in three stadiums across major cities.[^cite_note-585] Since 2020, the progress of women's integration into the Saudi sport scene began to develop rapidly.[^cite_note-586][^cite_note-587] 25 Saudi sport federations established a national women's team,[^cite_note-588] including a national [football](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saudi_Arabia_women's_national_football_team) and [basketball team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saudi_Arabia_women's_national_basketball_team). In November 2020, the [Saudi Arabian Football Federation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saudi_Arabian_Football_Federation) announced the launch of the first nationwide [Saudi women's premier league](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saudi_Women's_Premier_League).[^cite_note-589]

The nation has introduced many international sporting events. However, in August 2019, the kingdom's strategy received criticism for appearing as a method of [sportswashing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sportswashing_in_Saudi_Arabia) soon after Saudi's US-based 2018 lobbying campaign foreign registration documentations got published online. The documents showed Saudi Arabia as allegedly implementing a sportswashing strategy, including meetings and official calls with authorities of associations like [Major League Soccer](71802.md), [World Wrestling Entertainment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WWE_in_Saudi_Arabia), and the [National Basketball Association](22093.md).[^cite_note-590]

New York City: Facts on File. p. 1171. [ISBN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ISBN_\(identifier\)) [978-0-8160-5953-9](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special\:BookSources/978-0-8160-5953-9). Saudi Arabia is not totalitarian. Travel outside the country is common, political crimes and violence are rare, people are not in constant fear of the police, and the state does not try to take over all existing organizations, such as philanthropic, religious, commercial, and industrial groups. Saudi rulers still see themselves in a parental role, much like a sheikh of a tribe who is in close touch with the concerns of his tribesmen and keeps those concerns in balance. For so long a time, a relatively benign monarchy has ruled over this populace that it has become used to being looked after in this manner. Therefore, until recently, calls for a more open, representative political system did not win wide support.`{{cite book}}`: CS1 maint: multiple names: authors list ([link](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Category\:CS1_maint:_multiple_names:_authors_list))

[^cite_note-320]: &#x20;["Saudi Arabia: "It's like we are not human": Forced returns, abhorrent detention conditions of Ethiopian migrants in Saudi Arabia"](https://www.amnesty.org/en/documents/mde23/5826/2022/en/). *[Amnesty International](18947898.md)*. 16 December 2022. p. 6. [Archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20240314062320/https://www.amnesty.org/en/documents/mde23/5826/2022/en/) from the original on 14 March 2024. Retrieved 14 March 2024. Under Saudi Arabia's repressive sponsorship (kafala) system, migrant workers are only able to obtain a work permit or residency through an employer, and they are only able to terminate their contracts or change employers under certain conditions without the consent of their employer. Those who lose their residency status, either because they leave their jobs without the consent of the employer or overstay their work permits, and those who are unable to find employment in the formal sector are at risk of arbitrary detention, deportation to their home country by the authorities, as well as abuses such as forced labour and physical and sexual assault.
